- **deck-edit-preservation-and-source-sync**: Edit an existing slide deck without losing content and keep hand fixes alive across regeneration - record a per-slide preservation list of required text and objects before editing, diff the file against it afterwards and report every removed, moved or changed element, keep content source and layout template apart, write manual fixes back to the source, and warn which hand edits a regeneration would overwrite. Use when an AI edit of a pptx or HTML deck dropped a table, image or text, or small manual fixes vanished when the deck was regenerated. - **declausing**: Rewrite prose that reads as machine-written into prose that reads as written by someone who meant it — by applying Orwell's rules from "Politics and the English Language", excluding rule 5, so domain terms survive. Use when asked to de-Claude, de-AI, de-slop, unstuff, plain-language, or declause a draft; when an email, memo, README or PR body sounds structured rather than said; or before sending anything written for a human who will notice. - **declawed**: De-slop pass for any text: mechanically scans for the statistical tells of AI writing (the "not X but Y" reflex, puffery, uniform cadence, …) and rewrites by meaning into the target register, tweet to academic paper.
